=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an undirected tree rooted at node 0 with n nodes numbered from 0 to n - 1, represented by a 2D array edges of length n - 1, where edges[i] = [u_i, v_i, length_i] indicates an edge between nodes u_i and v_i with length length_i. You are also given an integer array nums, where nums[i] represents the value at node i.
A special path is defined as a downward path from an ancestor node to a descendant node such that all the values of the nodes in that path are unique.
Note that a path may start and end at the same node.
Return an array result of size 2, where result[0] is the length of the longest special path, and result[1] is the minimum number of nodes in all possible longest special paths.
 
Example 1:

Input: edges = [[0,1,2],[1,2,3],[1,3,5],[1,4,4],[2,5,6]], nums = [2,1,2,1,3,1]
Output: [6,2]
Explanation:
In the image below, nodes are colored by their corresponding values in nums

The longest special paths are 2 -> 5 and 0 -> 1 -> 4, both having a length of 6. The minimum number of nodes across all longest special paths is 2.

Example 2:

Input: edges = [[1,0,8]], nums = [2,2]
Output: [0,1]
Explanation:

The longest special paths are 0 and 1, both having a length of 0. The minimum number of nodes across all longest special paths is 1.

 
Constraints:

2 <= n <= 5 * 10^4
edges.length == n - 1
edges[i].length == 3
0 <= u_i, v_i < n
1 <= length_i <= 10^3
nums.length == n
0 <= nums[i] <= 5 * 10^4
The input is generated such that edges represents a valid tree.

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Now, is sliding window L = max(L, last_pos[nums[v]] + 1) enough to maintain uniqueness for all nodes in window? Let's formal proof:
Invariant after processing current node v: path_nodes[L..len-1] has all distinct values, and for any start s < L, path_nodes[s..len-1] has duplicate. Actually L is minimal valid start. When adding new node x at position p (len before), if last_pos[x] = q. Any valid window ending at new node must start > q to avoid duplicate x. Also must start >= old L because old window [oldL..p-1] was unique; if start < oldL, old segment invalid due some duplicate, and adding x cannot remove that duplicate. Thus start >= max(oldL, q+1). Conversely start = max(oldL, q+1) is valid: if max=oldL, then q < oldL, so old unique plus x no duplicate. If max=q+1, then q+1 >= oldL; segment [q+1..p-1] is suffix of unique old window, unique, and excludes q, so x unique. Good. This works.

        x = nums[v]
        prev_L[v] = L
        prev_last[v] = last_pos[x]
        pos = len(path_nodes)
        new_L = L
        p = last_pos[x]
        if p + 1 > new_L:
            new_L = p + 1
        L = new_L
        last_pos[x] = pos
        path_nodes.append(v)
        # evaluate
        length = dist[v] - dist[path_nodes[L]]
        nodes = len(path_nodes) - L
        if length > ans_len:
            ans_len = length
            ans_nodes = nodes

        for child, w in reversed(children[v]):
            stack.append((child, 0))

        # restore

Pseudo:

class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u,v,w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v,w))
            adj[v].append((u,w))
        max_val = max(nums)
        last_pos = [-1]*(max_val+1)
        dist = [0]*n
        depth_nodes = [0]*n
        prev_L = [0]*n
        prev_last = [-1]*n
        path_nodes = []
        L = 0
        ans_len = 0
        ans_nodes = 1
        stack = [(0, -1, 0)]  # node, parent, state (0 enter, 1 exit)
        while stack:
            v, p, state = stack.pop()
            if state == 0:
                x = nums[v]
                prev_L[v] = L
                prev_last[v] = last_pos[x]
                pos = len(path_nodes)
                # update L
                if last_pos[x] + 1 > L:
                    L = last_pos[x] + 1
                last_pos[x] = pos
                path_nodes.append(v)
                # evaluate
                length = dist[v] - dist[path_nodes[L]]
                cnt = len(path_nodes) - L
                if length > ans_len:
                    ans_len = length
                    ans_nodes = cnt
                elif length == ans_len and cnt < ans_nodes:
                    ans_nodes = cnt
                # push exit
                stack.append((v, p, 1))
                # push children
                for nb, w in adj[v]:
                    if nb == p:
                        continue
                    dist[nb] = dist[v] + w
                    depth_nodes[nb] = depth_nodes[v] + 1
                    stack.append((nb, v, 0))
            else:
                x = nums[v]
                last_pos[x] = prev_last[v]
                L = prev_L[v]
                path_nodes.pop()
        return [ans_len, ans_nodes]

Need check depth_nodes actually used? We don't use depth_nodes in this code except maybe not needed. We can remove depth_nodes. We only need dist. Path position is len(path_nodes). So no depth_nodes. Good. We don't need depth_nodes. Remove.

Need check dist for child set before push. But if child is pushed, and before processing, could dist be overwritten? No. But if using stack, a node is pushed exactly once. Good.

Potential issue: We push children in adjacency order. Since stack LIFO, children processed in reverse order. Fine.

Potential issue: We push exit before children. But we also push children by iterating adj[v]; if adj[v] includes parent, skip. Good.

Potential issue: `last_pos[x] + 1 > L` when last_pos[x] = -1, last_pos+1=0. If L=0, no change. Good.

Potential issue: `dist[path_nodes[L]]` if L is int position. path_nodes[L] exists. Good.

Our earlier example processed 2 then 5 then 3 then4; order different but ans same. Let's simulate with order maybe 4 first: path [0,1], L=0, last1=1. Process 2 val1: before path [0,1], L0, last1=0, new_L=1, append pos2, path [0,1,2], cand len dist2=2 - dist[path[1]=1]=1 =>1 nodes2 (1->2 values2,1). ans remains [2,3]. But is there path 1->2->? no. Longest length2 path 0->1->3 nodes3. Good.
What about path 0->1->2? values1,2,1 duplicate invalid. Good.

Now, a case where longest path starts not at root due duplicate:
chain 0-1 len1,1-2 len1,2-3 len1, nums [1,2,1,3].
Root cand0.
Node1 val2 cand len1 nodes2.
Node2 val1 duplicate root: L=1, cand len1 nodes2 (1->2).
Node3 val3: L=1, cand len2 nodes3 (1->2->3 values2,1,3). Longest length2 nodes3. Good.

Now, a case where L moves multiple times:
chain values [1,2,3,2,4,1,5]? Let's compute maybe.
At node3 val2 duplicate node1, L=2 (node2? positions 0 val1,1 val2,2 val3,3 val2 -> L=2). Path 2->3 length1 nodes2.
Node4 val4 L=2 path 2->3->4 length2 nodes3.
Node5 val1 duplicate root pos0, last1=0 < L=2, so L remains2. Path 2->3->4->5 values3,2,4,1 unique length3 nodes4. Good. If we had removed last1 when L moved, last1=-1, same. Good.
Node6 val5 L=2 path length4 nodes5. Good.
Now, if another val2 appears at node6, last2=3 (node3) >= L=2, new_L=4, path starts at node4? positions: 0 val1,1 val2,2 val3,3 val2,4 val4,5 val1,6 val2. L before node6=2. last2=3, new_L=4, path positions4..6 val4,val1,val2 unique length2 nodes3. Good.

Let's maybe write final code now:

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))
        
        max_val = max(nums)
        last_pos = [-1] * (max_val + 1)
        
        dist = [0] * n
        prev_L = [0] * n
        prev_last = [-1] * n
        
        path_nodes = []
        L = 0
        ans_len = 0
        ans_nodes = 1
        
        stack = [(0, -1, 0)]  # node, parent, state: 0 enter, 1 exit
        
        while stack:
            v, p, state = stack.pop()
            if state == 0:
                x = nums[v]
                prev_L[v] = L
                prev_last[v] = last_pos[x]
                
                pos = len(path_nodes)
                if last_pos[x] + 1 > L:
                    L = last_pos[x] + 1
                last_pos[x] = pos
                path_nodes.append(v)
                
                cur_len = dist[v] - dist[path_nodes[L]]
                cur_nodes = len(path_nodes) - L
                if cur_len > ans_len:
                    ans_len = cur_len
                    ans_nodes = cur_nodes
                elif cur_len == ans_len and cur_nodes < ans_nodes:
                    ans_nodes = cur_nodes
                
                stack.append((v, p, 1))
                for nb, w in adj[v]:
                    if nb == p:
                        continue
                    dist[nb] = dist[v] + w
                    stack.append((nb, v, 0))
            else:
                x = nums[v]
                last_pos[x] = prev_last[v]
                L = prev_L[v]
                path_nodes.pop()
        
        return [ans_len, ans_nodes]
```

Need check if `prev_L[v]` and `prev_last[v]` for root are set; yes. For exit, `x = nums[v]`; if v's value appears in last_pos, restore. Good.

Potential issue: In exit, we use `x = nums[v]` and restore `last_pos[x]`. But what if a descendant with same value x changed last_pos[x] and then restored to v's position; yes. But what if v's value x also appears in an ancestor and after entering v, last_pos[x] = pos_v. During processing children, if a child has same x, last_pos[x] changes and restores to pos_v. So at exit, last_pos[x] = pos_v. Restore to prev_last[v]. Good.

Potential issue: If a node v has value x and a child has same x, when child exits, last_pos[x] restored to pos_v. Good.

Potential issue: If a node v has value x and a descendant has same x, and the descendant is processed before v's exit; yes.

Potential issue: If a node v has value x and a descendant with same x causes L to move past v? Wait if descendant has same x, last_pos[x] before descendant is pos_v. Then new_L = max(L, pos_v+1). This means the valid window starting at descendant excludes v. After descendant exits, L restored to L after v. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def longestSpecialPath(self, edges: List[List[int]], nums: List[int]) -> List[int]:
        n = len(nums)
        adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
        for u, v, w in edges:
            adj[u].append((v, w))
            adj[v].append((u, w))

        max_val = max(nums)
        last_pos = [-1] * (max_val + 1)

        dist = [0] * n
        prev_L = [0] * n
        prev_last = [-1] * n

        path_nodes = []
        L = 0
        ans_len = 0
        ans_nodes = 1

        # stack entries: (node, parent, state), state 0 = enter, 1 = exit
        stack = [(0, -1, 0)]

        while stack:
            v, p, state = stack.pop()

            if state == 0:
                x = nums[v]
                prev_L[v] = L
                prev_last[v] = last_pos[x]

                pos = len(path_nodes)
                if last_pos[x] + 1 > L:
                    L = last_pos[x] + 1
                last_pos[x] = pos
                path_nodes.append(v)

                cur_len = dist[v] - dist[path_nodes[L]]
                cur_nodes = len(path_nodes) - L

                if cur_len > ans_len:
                    ans_len = cur_len
                    ans_nodes = cur_nodes
                elif cur_len == ans_len and cur_nodes < ans_nodes:
                    ans_nodes = cur_nodes

                stack.append((v, p, 1))
                for nb, w in adj[v]:
                    if nb == p:
                        continue
                    dist[nb] = dist[v] + w
                    stack.append((nb, v, 0))

            else:
                x = nums[v]
                last_pos[x] = prev_last[v]
                L = prev_L[v]
                path_nodes.pop()

        return [ans_len, ans_nodes]
```
